[document description]
The document is a list of records, one per line, each formatted as:

Records are 0-indexed and appear in order.

[question]

[root turn 1]
[assistant] This document is 5992 tokens — too long to read in one context. The result over a range does not depend on the order in which the document is read, so disjoint ranges can be computed independently and merged. The question asks for one total over the whole document, so the state is a single running sum — no per-grp breakdown is needed. I therefore split the range in half recursively, having a subagent compute the SUM of 'amt' over each half and combining the two partials. Once I have it for the whole document, I turn the combined result into the final answer.
Range 0..5992 is not below 500; splitting at midpoint 2996.
  -> CALLS: spawn_subagent({'subtask': "Over the records STARTING in tokens 0..2996, compute the SUM of 'amt'. When the range is less than 500 tokens, read it and compute the SUM of 'amt' over the records STARTING in the range. Put the result in \\boxed{}: the partial as a single integer."}); spawn_subagent({'subtask': "Over the records STARTING in tokens 2996..5992, compute the SUM of 'amt'. TASK synth_diff   (10 traces with this question shape)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[document description]
The document is a list of records, one per line, each formatted as:
  [<index>] id=<int> grp=<K1|K2|K3|K4|RST> amt=<signed int> flag=<Y|N>
Records are 0-indexed and appear in order.

[question]
Each record has a signed integer 'amt' and a 'flag' (Y or N). What is the SUM of 'amt' over flag=Y records MINUS the SUM over flag=N records? Give the single integer in \boxed{}.

[root turn 1]
[assistant] This document is 5992 tokens — too long to read in one context. The result over a range does not depend on the order in which the document is read, so disjoint ranges can be computed independently and merged. The question subtracts one flag's total from the other's, so the state keeps a running total per flag. I therefore split the range in half recursively, having a subagent compute the per-flag totals of 'amt' (a running sum for flag=Y and for flag=N) over each half and combining the two partials. Once I have it for the whole document, I turn the combined result into the final answer.
Range 0..5992 is not below 500; splitting at midpoint 2996.
  -> CALLS: spawn_subagent({'subtask': "Over the records STARTING in tokens 0..2996, compute the per-flag totals of 'amt' (a running sum for flag=Y and for flag=N). Recursively split the range at its midpoint, delegating each half to a subagent, until the range is less than 500 tokens. When the range is less than 500 tokens, read it and compute the per-flag totals of 'amt' (a running sum for flag=Y and for flag=N) over the records STARTING in the range. Put the result in \\boxed{}: the partial as `<flag>=<total>` entries joined by `|`, where `<flag>` is Y or N; `none` if no record starts in the range."}); spawn_subagent({'subtask': "Over the records STARTING in tokens 2996..5992, compute the per-flag totals of 'amt' (a running sum for flag=Y and for flag=N). Recursively split the range at its midpoint, delegating each half to a subagent, until the range is less than 500 tokens. When the range is less than 500 tokens, read it and compute the per-flag totals of 'amt' (a running sum for flag=Y and for flag=N) over the records STARTING in the range. Put the result in \\boxed{}: the partial as `<flag>=<total>` entries joined by `|`, where `<flag>` is Y or N; `none` if no record starts in the range."})
[tool:spawn_subagent] N=-162|Y=-46
[tool:spawn_subagent] N=-27|Y=-157

[root turn 2]
[assistant] Merge (add per-flag) my two children's tallies (above) -> N=-189|Y=-203.
flag=Y total = -203, flag=N total = -189; difference = (-203) - (-189) = -14.
\boxed{-14}

followed by one or more paragraphs of body text (real prose). Entries are numbered in order starting at 1. The src values are listed above in their natural order.

[question]
Go through the entries in order, keeping a running count of entries with tag=K4. What is the number of the FIRST entry at which that running count reaches 6? If it never reaches 6, answer none. Give the entry number (or none) in \boxed{}.

[root turn 1]
[assistant] This document is 5640 tokens — too long to read in one context. This task is order-dependent: the answer is the FIRST entry at which a running count reaches 6, which depends on every entry before it, so the document has to be processed sequentially, in document order. The answer is the entry at which the running count of tag=K4 entries first reaches 6, so the accumulator carries the running count and that entry once it is reached — the count alone forgets when it happened. I therefore process the document from the start with a running accumulator: read the first slice and update the accumulator by counting tag=K4 entries in order and noting the first entry at which the count reaches 6, then hand the rest of the document plus the accumulator to a subagent to continue the same way. Once the final slice is folded in, the accumulator holds the whole-document result and I turn it into the final answer.
Reading the first 400 tokens (0..400) of this range to fold, then the next 200 tokens to finish the last entry's body (it may run well past 400).
  -> CALLS: read_chunk({'start': 0, 'end': 400}); read_chunk({'start': 400, 'end': 600})
[tool:read_chunk] …
[tool:read_chunk] …

[root turn 2]
[assistant] The last entry's body is still cut off at 600 (no blank line / next header yet), so I read the next 200 tokens (600..800) to finish it.
  -> CALLS: read_chunk({'start': 600, 'end': 800})
[tool:read_chunk] …

[root turn 3]
[assistant] Folding the 2 entries whose HEADER STARTS in 0..400 into the accumulator (in order):
- entry 1 (tag=K1) (no) → count=0
- entry 2 (tag=K2) (no) → count=0
→ accumulator = count=0. Delegating the rest 400..5640 with the updated accumulator.
  -> CALLS: spawn_subagent({'subtask': 'Continue the running accumulator by counting tag=K4 entries in order and noting the first entry at which the count reaches 6, over the entries STARTING in tokens 400..5640.\naccumulator so far = count=0\nRead the first 400 tokens and update the accumulator over the entries STARTING in tokens 400..5640, then delegate the rest of the range to one subagent with the updated accumulator. When the range is less than 400 tokens, process it directly and return the final accumulator.'})
[tool:spawn_subagent] count=6|first=15

[root turn 4]
[assistant] The chain returned its final accumulator.
The cumulative count of tag=K4 entries first reaches 6 at entry 15.
\boxed{15}

TASK vt_novel   (1 traces with this question shape)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
[document description]
The document is a long passage of text with short 'VAR ... = ...' assignment lines hidden inside it, in order. Answer the question using ONLY this passage.

[question]
Memorize and track the chains of variable assignment hidden in the text (a line 'VAR A = VAR B' copies B's current value into A). Keep a running picture of every variable's current value as you go. Which VARs finish with the value 45575? Give all of them, comma-separated. Answer with the list inside \boxed{}.

[root turn 1]
[assistant] This document is 6488 tokens — too long to read in one context. This task is order-dependent: a `VAR A = VAR B` line copies B's value AT THAT POINT, and a variable can be reassigned later, so the document has to be processed sequentially, in document order. The question asks which VARs finish with the value 45575, but that is only known at the end and a value reaches a VAR through copies, so the accumulator carries the current value of EVERY variable seen so far — not just the VARs currently holding 45575 (a later assignment overwrites an earlier one). I therefore process the document from the start with a running accumulator: read the first slice and update the accumulator by applying each VAR assignment in order (VAR A = VAR B copies B's current value), then hand the rest of the document plus the accumulator to a subagent to continue the same way. Once the final slice is folded in, the accumulator holds the whole-document result and I turn it into the final answer.
Reading the first 400 tokens (0..400) of this range to fold, then the next 200 tokens to finish the last record (its line may run past 400).
  -> CALLS: read_chunk({'start': 0, 'end': 400}); read_chunk({'start': 400, 'end': 600})
[tool:read_chunk] …
[tool:read_chunk] …

[root turn 2]
